So $N_2 = C \cdot 10^{14} + E \cdot 10^6 + A^R$.
$S(C) + S(E) + S(A^R) = 91$. Note $S(A^R) = S(A)$.
So $S(C) + S(E) + S(A) = 91$.

$N_3$: 20 digits. Sum = 109.
Let first 6 digits of $N_3$ be $F$.
Let remaining 14 digits of $N_3$ be $G$.
$S(F) + S(G) = 109$.
Last 6 digits of $N_3$ = reverse of first 6 digits of $N_2$. So $G$ ends with $C^R$.

$B = 61111111111111$. Sum = 19.
$N_1 = 99999961111111111111$. Length 20. Sum = 54 + 19 = 73. Correct.

We need $S(N_3) = 109$. So remaining 14 digits must sum to $109 - 10 = 99$.
